cliEval release: the declarative layer that turns YAML manifests into executable, type-safe eval specifications and provides the assertion DSL that Phase 3's runner will invoke. It builds six new Python modules / schema files under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/`, extends `commands.py` with two informational subcommands (`eval list`, `eval describe`), and adds three test modules under `tests/cli/test_eval/`.\n\nThe 15 evals (E1-E15) defined in the design spec are authored declaratively as YAML per decision D-4 (YAML primary + Python callback escape hatch). This phase produces the parsing, validation, parameter-expansion, template-variable resolution, and assertion-DSL machinery that makes those manifests executable in Phase 3. Without this phase the orchestrator has nothing to schedule and the runner has nothing to assert against.\n\n## Key Objectives\n\nThe following objectives MUST be achieved by this task:\n\n1. **Type-safe data models:** `EvalSpec`, `EvalResult`, `RunSummary`, `ExpectFailure`, `ExpectResult`, and `EvalContext` dataclasses live in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/models.py` with round-trip-safe serialization and clear invariants.\n2. **YAML manifest loader:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/loader.py` parses suite YAML, validates against the JSON Schema, expands `parameterize:` blocks into separate `EvalSpec` instances (e.g., `E2.1`, `E2.2`, `E2.3`), and resolves the documented template variables (`{session_id}`, `{project_key}`, `{now - <duration>}`, `{home}`, `{eval_id}`). Invalid manifests raise `ManifestError` with file:line context.\n3. **Expect.* assertion DSL:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py` provides the `Expect` class with sub-builders (`FileExpect`, `JsonlExpect`, `SettingsExpect`, `ExitCodeExpect`, `StreamExpect`, `DurationExpect`) per design-spec §8; each builder returns an `ExpectCallable` of shape `(EvalContext) -> ExpectResult`.\n4. **Manifest schema + example:** A draft 2020-12 JSON Schema at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/suite.schema.json` plus an authoring guide `suites/README.md` and a 2-eval minimal-but-valid `suites/example.yaml` (NOT real.yaml — that is a downstream workstream).\n5. **CLI surface — informational subcommands:** Extend `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py` with `eval list` (enumerates `suites/*.yaml` with name/version/description/eval-count) and `eval describe --suite SUITE [--eval ID]` (prints parsed manifest content for the whole suite or a single eval).\n6. **Test coverage:** Three test files — `test_models.py`, `test_loader.py`, `test_expect.py` — exercising dataclass invariants, valid/invalid manifest parsing, `parameterize:` expansion, template-variable substitution, and the PASS/FAIL paths for every Expect.* builder with observed-vs-expected failure messages.\n7. **All eleven acceptance criteria green:** AC-P2.1 through AC-P2.11 from the BUILD_REQUEST must each be satisfied and traceable to a specific checklist item that produces or verifies their outputs.\n\n## Prerequisites & Dependencies\n\n### Parent Task & Dependencies\n- **Parent Task:** cliEval-release (Phase 2 of 4 implementation phases per design-spec §17)\n- **Blocking Dependencies:**\n  - `TASK-RF-20260518-cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates`: provides `cli/eval/__init__.py` (exporting `eval_group`), `cli/eval/commands.py` (the `eval_group` Click group skeleton + `eval doctor` subcommand), `cli/eval/pty/` vendored driver, `cli/eval/isolation.py` (`HomeIsolation`), and `cli/eval/capability_gates.py`.\n\n**CRITICAL — P1 must be MERGED to master before this task's execution begins.** This task extends `cli/eval/commands.py` (adds `eval list` and `eval describe` subcommands to the existing `eval_group` Click group) and presumes the `cli/eval/` sub-package layout exists. The review\nis a *read-only* re-verification of the pinned upstream SHA; resync work is\nspun out into its own task only when the checklist surfaces a security- or\nbehavior-relevant change. The default outcome of a clean review is to roll the\nreview anchor forward 90 days and record the row in `PROVENANCE.md` §4.\n\nA review may be triggered out-of-band (i.e. before the next scheduled date) by\nany of:\n\n- A CVE published against `pexpect` or `ptytest` upstream.\n- An upstream commit touching the prompt-detection or ANSI-stripping code paths.\n- A failing CI run on `tests/cli/eval/test_pty_vendor.py` after an unrelated\n  refactor (signal that the pin has implicitly diverged from on-disk content).\n\n## Step 1 — Confirm the current pin\n\n1. Open `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` and copy the **Pinned\n   upstream SHA** from §2.\n2. Confirm the SHA matches the upstream commit it claims by visiting\n   `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/commit/<pinned-sha>` and\n   verifying the commit page loads (i.e. upstream has not force-pushed the SHA\n   out of existence).\n3. If the SHA no longer resolves upstream, **escalate to a resync task\n   immediately** — a missing pin is a release blocker, not a quarterly review\n   item.\n\n## Step 2 — Fetch the current upstream HEAD\n\n1. Query `GET https://api.github.com/repos/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/commits/master`\n   (or equivalent) and record the current upstream HEAD SHA and commit date.\n2. If `upstream HEAD == pinned SHA`, jump to Step 5 (no drift; record clean\n   review).\n3. Otherwise, record the diff range `<pinned-sha>..<head-sha>` for Step 3.\n\n## Step 3 — Triage the upstream diff\n\n1. Read the upstream diff range from Step 2 via\n   `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/compare/<pinned>...<head>`\n   (or `git log --oneline <pinned>..<head>` against an upstream clone).\n2. Classify each upstream commit into one of:\n   - **Security** — CVE fix, sandbox escape, command-injection patch.\n   - **Behavior** — change to prompt detection, ANSI stripping, line buffering,\n     timeout semantics, `pexpect.spawn` argument handling.\n   - **Maintenance** — docs, CI, formatting, dependency floors, test-only changes.\n3. Any **Security** classification escalates to a resync task in the same\n   review pass. **Behavior** classifications require a judgment call from the\n   review owner (resync if our consumers — `PtyDriver`, `PtyStream` — depend on\n   the changed surface; defer otherwise with a recorded rationale).\n   **Maintenance** classifications never trigger a resync.\n\n## Step 4 — Re-verify license + attribution alignment\n\n1. Diff the upstream `LICENSE` file at the new HEAD against\n   `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`. They MUST be byte-identical text\n   (whitespace included) for the pin to remain valid.\n2. If the upstream LICENSE text changed:\n   - Update `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` in the same commit as any\n     SHA resync.\n   - Re-verify the canonical attribution clause in the top-level `NOTICE`\n     against any upstream LICENSE text changes; update both `NOTICE` and the\n     D-10 ADR body in `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md` if the\n     clause moved.\n3. If the upstream LICENSE text is unchanged but the pin is being held (no\n   resync), record this verification in the review log row anyway — it is the\n   AC10 commitment.\n\n## Step 5 — Record the review outcome\n\n1. Append a row to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` §4 with:\n   - **Date** of review (`YYYY-MM-DD`).\n   - **Reviewer** name.\n   - **Upstream SHA at review** (the HEAD captured in Step 2).\n   - **Outcome** — one of: *Pin held* / *Resync scheduled* / *Resync landed*.\n   - **Notes** — 1-line summary of the triage call (e.g. *\"3 maintenance\n     commits upstream; no behavior drift; pin held.\"*).\n2. If outcome is *Pin held*: bump the **Next review due** date in §3 by 90 days\n   from today.\n3. If outcome is *Resync scheduled* or *Resync landed*: open / link a tracked\n   resync task, follow the §3 *Resync procedure* steps, and set **Next review\n   due** based on whether the resync is in the same window (keep anchor) or\n   carries past (bump 90 days from the resync landing date).\n\n---\n\n**Acceptance:** This checklist satisfies AC10 (fork SHA pin + drift policy). It\nis intentionally a *procedure* document, not an automation. Automation of the\nquarterly trigger (e.g. a calendar reminder or a CI job that opens an issue\n90 days after the last review row) is out of scope for T02.03 and may be added\nlater under a separate R5-mitigation task (per `T02.26` notes in\n`phase-2-tasklist.md`).\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4009,"cache_read_input_tokens":111461,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4009,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0943034e-d96f-42e8-88e6-13d16c10b25d","uuid":"c6239648-7479-4f03-a7b9-d84f5f1f747e"}

Why this deliverable exists\n\nAC10 of the cliEval design contract requires the vendored `ptytest` fork to\nhave:\n\n1. A **frozen upstream SHA** recorded at a known path with the vendoring date.\n2. A **quarterly review cadence** captured at the same path, with a named\n   owner.\n3. A **standing review procedure** that the owner can execute without\n   reconstructing context every time.\n4. An **explicit resync path** so that a clean review (the common case) and a\n   resync (the rare case) are both unambiguous.\n\nWithout (3) and (4), the cadence in (2) becomes lore that decays the moment\nthe maintainer rotates.
